REPRESENTATIVE REPORT ON THE WORK OF THE PROTECTION OF HUMAN RIGHTS OF THE REPUBLIC OF SLOVENIA IN THE YEAR 2012 The 18th periodic report was submitted to the Ombudsman for consideration. On this occasion, I give the floor first to the Ombudsman of Human Rights, Ms Vlasta Nussdorfer, to further explain the report.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u2	Dear President of the National Assembly, Mr Janko Weber, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! In the first days of July, I handed over the 18th report on the work of the Ombudsman of the Republic of Slovenia for 2012 to the President of the National Assembly, the President of the Republic and the President of the Government. The State Council of the Republic of Slovenia is then 18. 9. 2013 supported the Ombudsman's efforts and his proposals and recommendations to the Government of the Republic of Slovenia and proposed that they be taken into account as far as possible. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia is 3. Ten. 2013 submitted its response report to our annual report as well as information on the implementation of the recommendations of the National Assembly at the time of consideration of the previous 17-year report. The comprehensive review of the report was 4. Ten. 2013 to our home Commission for petitions and for human rights and equal opportunities under the chairmanship of Ambassador Eva Irgl. Today, I thank the National Assembly for its extremely rapid consideration of the Ombudsman's extensive report for last year, 2012, with another consideration at the beginning of the year, January, for 2011. With the Deputy and three Deputys, I took over immediately after taking office, as this is a report on the work of the Ombudsman Dr. Zdenka Čebašek Travnik. We are in an extremely important year when we celebrate the 20th anniversary of the Law on the Ombudsman. Next year, ending with the anniversary of the operation of the first Magi Ombudsman. Biziak, we would like to review the implementation of all the Ombudsman's recommendations so far. Only then will there be a clear path of protection of human rights in the Republic of Slovenia and guidance for the third decade of the Ombudsman's operation. Although statistics are not the only indicator of work, it is not possible to overlook the 3722 cases dealt with last year, which represents a 21% increase. For various reasons, especially because of the crisis situation, we are also following this year the trend of increasing both initiatives and calls that have already reached the entire level of last September. There has also been an increase in out-of-seas operations, which is of exceptional importance to the Ombudsman. The law requires me to report to the respected assembly on work, findings, respect for human rights and fundamental freedoms and on the legal security of citizens in the Republic of Slovenia. I can find out, and I wrote this in the introduction to the report that the rule of law is this moment, today, this year for a great trial, because many people in Slovenia are also in violation of their rights to effective remedies. The rights of people in Slovenian courts are still not always judged within a reasonable time frame despite truly tangible progress. The principles of good governance are not respected. The authorities of the State and the crisis relations are not always sufficient reason to adopt rules and laws across professions and civil society, and often by knowingly violating fundamental standards for the protection of human rights. The Ombudsman has repeatedly highlighted the longevity of administrative, in particular appeals, procedures in his annual reports, as well as the absence of an explanation of the duty of administrative authorities. Significant progress has not yet been seen in this area, but we certainly expect it. The social state is the principle of erosion. Social security is diminishing, including due to past mistakes in the country and the wealth of unstoppable greed for corruption, and the inadmissible and often unpunished place of social property. The laws adopted also define control mechanisms, but they are not effective enough or not working at all, and people are demanding it. If I focus on the 2012 report, the first three places among the initiatives under consideration are in the area of social security, judicial and police procedures followed and then constitutional rights initiatives followed. In the area of constitutional rights, we have dealt mainly with hate speech, and we have recommended to all those who participate in public debates, the media and the public at large that they do not promote hatred and intolerance on the basis of any personal circumstances. While it is essential to weigh between several human rights, also those to freedom of expression, which enjoys special protection in accordance with the established practice of the European Court of Human Rights. It should be stressed that the Ombudsman cannot judge — this is often expected of us — whether there are also signs of a crime in a particular case. There are a number of dilemmas related to hate speech in forums and the anonymity of authors. The Ombudsman recommended that the Government consider the possibility of publicly promoting hatred, violence or intolerance as an offence. The Ombudsman proposes that serious consideration be given to the possibility of a civil penalty or compensation for unjustified interference with privacy through public publication. In the area of electoral law, we have identified the absence of effective legal remedies and therefore the Ombudsman recommends that the Government examine the possibility of procedures for the approval of alternate members of municipal councils, in particular the judicial protection of candidates for alternate members in cases of inaction or obstruction of the mayor and other authorities of the municipality, and propose such amendments to the legislation that will prevent detected irregularities, abuse of electoral procedures and inefficiencies of legal remedies in the procedures for the election of representatives of local interests to the Council of State. In the area of privacy and personal data protection, we have found that the misuse of archival material also makes it possible to disregard the Law on the Protection of Documentary and Archive Material and Archives. The Ombudsman has made a request to the Constitutional Court to assess the constitutionality of the Law on the Protection of Documentary and Archive Material and Archives because it does not regulate the extradition and accessibility of materials from psychiatric institutions containing sensitive personal data on the treatment of individuals. The following is the area of discrimination, which calls attention to the need to establish an independent advocate who, in accordance with the EU acquis, will ensure that cases of violations of the prohibition of discrimination are dealt with impartially, effectively and independently in all areas. It is also recommended that an independent institution for the protection and promotion of human rights be set up to act on the basis of the so-called Paris principles. The report pays attention to national and ethnic minorities and to issues related to the Hungarian, Italian and Roma communities and to their specific rights in the Constitution. In particular, I would like to point out that the Ombudsman issued a special report on the living situation of Roma in the South-East Slovenia area. I expect the Government to develop a strategy to address this issue and to burden local communities and civil society. Situations in environments where the Roma community and the majority of the population share together threaten the realization of human and special rights of the Roma community, as well as the realization of human rights of the inhabitants of these communities. We have found a violation of the right of access to drinking water and sanitation. Local communities, authorities and the State are obliged to take measures to address these violations. We believe that the State must not avoid being held responsible for the elimination of human rights violations when the local community fails. I am moving on to the area of restrictions on personal freedom, where we have dealt with the initiatives of detainees, convicts, as well as some juveniles from the educational home. There will be spatial distress in some institutions, their overcrowding. It shall take care of the report on the lack of financial resources and also of professional staff. When we speak of persons deprived of their liberty, we cannot go beyond the role of the Ombudsman as a special state preventive mechanism. Following the Act on the ratification of the Optional Protocol to the Convention against Torture and Other Cruel, Inhuman or Degrading Punishment or the conduct of guardians in cooperation with non-governmental organisations and experts, it shall monitor places where people are deprived of their liberty or of their limited movement and other tasks under the mandate. We made 46 visits, of which 37 were unannounced. This year we are chairman of Southeast Europe, which is certainly a recognition of our work so far. In June 2012, we welcomed an open unit for forensic psychiatry in Maribor, which was also established on the Ombudsman's initiative. We have dealt with persons with mental disabilities, persons in social security institutions, as well as with special protective measures in health and social institutions, and have pointed out the implementation of the educational measure of the training institution. In these areas we still find human rights violations. In the area of justice, we have highlighted two major problems: judicial backlogs and quality of trial at first instance. The trial within a reasonable time is one of the fundamental human rights. On the other hand, we have drawn attention to the problems of judicial experts and free legal aid, which for many because of a few square metres of land or a few euros is too unavailable and necessary for them. We have also paid a lot of attention to enforcement problems and the free choice of enforcement assets, regardless of the level of the creditor's claim. In view of the initiatives taken, we have also encountered violations, the amendment of the Law on Offences, the replacement of the fine with tasks of general benefit, as well as the decision on the requirements for judicial protection, where human rights must be respected. There was no delay in the prosecution's work, which was encouraging, but we wondered about an effective remedy for the injured person in the event of a disagreement with the decision of the state prosecutor. We considered that each investor should receive at least the first response to its first role, especially if the case in the prosecution is archived and ad act given. Although the number of initiatives has decreased in the field of lawyering, there is still evidence of non-disclosure, non-refoulement of documentation, as well as payment of costs and accounts contrary to oral agreement. Disciplinary procedures are still too long. Many instigators were also disappointed with the insurance company’s responses, which often rejected claims for compensation. There were no initiatives in the area of notariat, and future changes to this regime must further increase the legal certainty of users. There was no increase in initiatives in the area of police procedures. The police have carried out many activities to demobilize procedures and relieve the work of police officers, which is essential. We have been monitoring police action in protests at the end of 2012 and have pointed out the duty of appropriate action, but consistent respect for the dignity of the detainees. The police have always responded to the Ombudsman's recommendations, which have focused on consistent respect for human rights. I'm moving to administrative matters. The practice of a number of public administrations has become a failure to respect deadlines. In our recommendation to the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Public Administration, we have proposed that it be ensured that all public sector bodies comply with the provisions of the Administrative Regulation and comply with their explanatory duty within 15 days. Similarly, the said Ministry should establish levers to respect all instructions in decisions in the administrative procedure. We have discussed initiatives relating to procedures relating to the acquisition of rights from public funds, as well as to the slow and ineffective conduct of inspection procedures. We have highlighted the issue of foreign citizens, the area of international protection and the elimination of free legal aid for applicants for international protection. The process of denationalisation remains open, and we do not see any reason for this process to finally end. There is also no consistent compliance with the deadlines for deciding on complaints in the field of taxes and customs. Some unresolved property matters with the congregations are in the eyes, as individuals have allowed public roads or roads to pass through their land, and the congregation does not provide a dose to the affected, does not offer compensation, or is very low. The correction of the injustices also referred to the application of the ZUJF. The Constitutional Court has confirmed our arguments that some have established Articles 143 of the ZUJF in contravention of the Constitution. We have applied for a constitutional assessment as soon as it has been implemented on the basis of the initiatives of many associations and pension societies, as well as more than 250 bitter individuals. The Constitutional Court has repealed the non-constitutional provisions of the ZUJF. I reiterate the consequences of unacceptable regulatory changes and the financial consequences of non-constitutional acts. Just think, honourable Members and Members, how long they have spent and will be dealt with by various bodies, to decide on the complaints lodged, without having to mention in particular the lost time and impact and dignity of individuals and their families. We have discussed the issue of regulating war and hidden graves, where there is a lack of funds and therefore the work of the government commission has been suspended. We'll have to solve the problems. The Ombudsman in this area proposes that the Government provide adequate financial resources for the detection of hidden war and post-war graves, and that appropriate symbolic burial of the victim, memorial signs and access of the relatives of the victims to the burial site have already been discovered. In the field of social activities, we have dealt with both pre-school education and higher and higher education, standards and standards changes, and also with the reduction of resources for higher education and science. We have proposed that the Ministry of Education, Science and Sport, in possible integration, closure and other reorganisations of secondary schools, and in reducing the entry points, should carry out these procedures on the basis of objective analyses, involving all interested parties, in particular transparent ones, so that there is no public perception of arbitration and the neglect of certain regions in education. We encourage all competent authorities to make very thoughtful and responsible changes in the field of education, science and sport and to have far-reaching consequences for possible early decisions. With many non-governmental organisations in the field of environment and space, we wonder what land we will leave to the craftsmen and whether capital really has an advantage over health. Public participation in environmental and spatial interventions, regulated award of concessions, consistent work of inspection services, recognition of noise, smell and pollution with dangerous PM10 particles is essential. The criteria for determining the order of treatment of the individual application or the priorities of the part of the inspection shall be clearly laid down. In the area of the municipal economy, the largest initiatives were due to the payment and cost of services for the provision of individual municipal services, as well as the exercise of the chimney service and the right to water as a universal human right. It is necessary to amend the Law on Funeral and Funeral Activities and on the Regulation of Cemeteries. Unfortunately, people have lost confidence in the country and those institutions that have powers, powers and responsibilities. The social situation is getting worse. Housing matters are closely linked to the property situation and social situation of the individual. It is necessary to regulate the housing policy of the State and the municipalities and to adopt a national housing programme. It will still be a violation of the rights of tenants of denationalised dwellings and a failure to respect the findings of the European Social Rights Committee at the Council of Europe. In the field of labour relations, we have considered non-payment of salaries, non-payment of social security contributions, suffering, harassment, bullying, volunteering, agency workers' chains, pensions and scholarships. Among other things, we have requested that the conditions for the operation of the work-providing agencies and the entry into the records of the competent ministry be tightened. Inspection should be strengthened and offenders punished more severely. The Public Finance Balance Act has been, and is, in part, in the Constitutional Opinion, which shows that all possible consequences, in particular in practice and in the issuance of all sub-laws that normally come with too long a delay, should be foreseen before amending legislation affecting human rights. There is still no new health legislation. It is essential to include the interested professional public and service users in the proposed solutions. I cannot overlook the growing financial and economic crisis, which is changing legislation and, as a result, shows an increasing risk to people. The Ombudsman constantly points to the rights of the socially weak, from children to the elderly. An active employment policy is essential, a faster response to the needs of the unemployed and those who still have jobs, the preparation of programmes that will ease the growing increase in social transfers, which represent the elimination of many fires. It is necessary to invest in development and knowledge, and to give priority to man and to the quality of his life. For man is our only wealth, not the financial institutions that are this instant cancer wound our society. In this area, we are pleased with all the positive responses of the ministry to alleviate the crisis. Our society and its progress will not be without children who must not share the fate of parents who cannot spend the month without the assistance of the state and charities. We've paid attention to all their children, all their problems. We also had a conference on this subject and also drew attention to the child in the media. We're in the month we recorded, and so I conclude, the day of the elderly, the week of the child, the day of the fight against human trafficking, the day of the white rod. All vulnerable people deserve attention. Thank you very much for your attention. Marie Curie wrote: "They taught me that the path of progress is not easy and not short, but I will faithfully follow it with my fellow workers." Thank you very much.

At its 14th meeting, the Commission considered the 18th annual report of the Ombudsman for 2012 for petitions and for human rights and equal opportunities. The representative of the proposer, Ms Vlasta Nussdorfer, provided an additional explanation for the report in which she introduced his structure and statistics, cooperation with NGOs and field visits. The highest number of cases in 2012 was in the area of social security, followed by judicial and police procedures and constitutional rights. In the context of the hate speech, many comments were made regarding the insult of the religious feelings of citizens. At the same time, it stressed the important role of detection and prosecution bodies. The Ombudsman also recommends exploring the possibility of publicly promoting hatred, violence or intolerance being penalised as an offence. She pointed out the potential for misuse of archival material, which would also allow non-compliance with the Law on the Protection of Documentary and Archive Materials and Archives. The Ombudsman also points out that the Office for Equal Opportunities has been abolished and that the Interdepartmental Working Group on Human Rights has been disbanded. She responded to initiatives concerning letters that insult the Hungarian national community and also focused her attention on the living situation of Roma in south-eastern Slovenia, disability issues, children's rights and everything else, as she has already discussed in the introduction today. The representative of the Council of State briefly presented the Council's written opinion on the report, which he considered to be transparent and well structured with the recommendations and proposals made. Among other things, he touched on the areas of election of representatives of local interests to the Council of State or the lack of legislation that does not prevent local authorities from using electoral practices. He also drew attention to lengthy and complex procedures for the categorisation of municipal roads, to the right to be tried within a reasonable time frame in the field of social security, judicial and police proceedings. In a short presentation, the representative of the Ministry of Justice stressed that the Government of the Republic of Slovenia is considering the Ombudsman's reports and appreciates his work. The ministries and government services are responding to the infringements. The government’s response report to the Ombudsman’s 2012 regular annual report presents concrete responses to the cases under consideration and proposals for the implementation of the recommendations. The representative of the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities stressed that the Ministry is trying to respond to people's difficulties in the light of the current situation and is working with the Ombudsman whose recommendations have been thoroughly examined. By means of various forms of assistance, they are trying to help people in social distress, explaining that more European funds will be made available. A representative of the Supreme State Prosecutor's Office, a representative of the Supreme Court and a representative of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia for nationality, then spoke. In the discussion below, the members and members of the Commission gave their views and conclusions on the content of the report, to which individual representatives of the institutions invited to the meeting also responded. Among other things, it has been said that some of the Ombudsman’s findings continue to be repeated. Among other things, the debaters touched on the ethics of the public word. In this context, the Ombudsman's recommendation to the public debates constitutes a continuation of the warnings that have already been transmitted, for example, by the European Commission to combat racism and discrimination. Among other things, the debate also drew attention to the non-response of some authorities. The word was about tolerance for those who thought differently, about violations of the right to a healthy environment. The area of economic crime in connection with human rights violations has also been highlighted. Attention was also paid to the five victims of postwar beatings and to the relevant group memorial markings, as well as to means of regulating hidden graves. In view of the current situation, there has also been talk of increasing unemployment and the social pressures of people. The member and members of the Commission voted on the proposal for a recommendation to the Ombudsman's report and adopted it by 6 votes in favour and none against. The text of the proposal for a recommendation was put to the vote in full with the preamble and explanation adopted by the members and members of the Commission. An integral part of this report is a proposal for a recommendation drawn up by the Commission as a working body, and today it will also be addressed by the National Assembly. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u5	Thank you, too. I give the floor to the representative of the Government of Mrs Tina Brecelj, State Secretary of the Ministry of Justice.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u6	Thank you so much for saying that, Mr. President. Dear Lady Guardian, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! The Government of the Republic of Slovenia welcomes and accepts the Ombudsman's recommendations for improvement and notes that despite the economic and financial crisis, ministries and government services are responsive and committed to the resolution of violations. The Government's opinion presents concrete responses to the cases raised by the Ombudsman and proposals for the implementation of the recommendations, which are also partly covered by the measures already taken to implement the recommendations of last year's report. Let me introduce a few concrete responses briefly. There has been an agreement between the judiciary and the executive, which contains concrete measures and obligations both between the judicial and executive branches of government with a view to shortening judicial proceedings and ensuring a reasonable period of time. The administrative authorities also actively seek to speed up the resolution of administrative procedures and to respect the decision-making deadlines. In order to rationalise and improve the decision-making and functioning of inspection services, work of certain authorities has been reorganised or even merged into single services, such as the Office of the Republic of Slovenia for Safe Food, Veterinary and Plant Protection. Solutions are being drawn up to better regulate the institution's advocate of the principle of equality. The Interdepartmental Commission on Human Rights has been re-established. The Ministry of Justice is actively addressing the problem of prison overcrowding and the general improvement of prison conditions. In preparation, the Law on free legal assistance aims at simplifying and expediting the procedure for obtaining free legal aid and the amendment to the Law on Enforcement and Insurance with the aim of increasing the effectiveness of enforcement procedures. A new proposal for a family code is already in preparation, taking full account of the benefits of children. The Council of Europe Convention on the Protection of Children against Sexual Exploitation and Sexual Abuse has been ratified. The Ministry of Foreign Affairs shall endeavour to ratify as soon as possible the Optional Protocol to the UN Convention on the Rights of the Child, the UN Conventions on Enforced Disappearances and the Council of Europe Conventions on Violence against Women. In order to protect the Roma community, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia will organise a public debate as a starting point for the drafting of a new law on the Roma community. The newly adopted Labour Relations Act and the amendment of the CFPIPP strengthens certain rights and the situation of workers in cases of infringements by employers. In December 2012, a new law on pension and disability insurance was adopted to fully implement the Constitutional Court’s decision. The Ministry of Health is preparing a new Law on Health Care and Health Insurance, a new Law on Patients' Rights and three new laws – Health Act, the Law on the Abduction and Transplantation of Parts of the Human Body for Treatment and the Law on Medicinal Products. Measures are being prepared to simplify and accelerate procedures for the issue of water licences, measures to improve air quality and regulation of the waste management area. The ministries have taken concrete action to implement the Ombudsman's reports, while at the same time clarifying which recommendations they may disagree with or assess that they are satisfactorily regulated in positive law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u8	Thank you for your word, Mr. President. Madam Guardian, her Deputy, Minister and Minister, Secretary of State and Secretary of State, Members and Members of Parliament! The Ombudsman's report is always a moment for the Parliamentary Group of Social Democrats to reflect on the state of human rights in Slovenia. Judging from the 18th report of the Ombudsman, we need to find the sad fact that the number of posts in the Ombudsman is increasing. This is not surprising in view of the situation in which we are living when social pressures are on the rise, as well as the dissatisfaction of people with social and political conditions. When I talk about the state of human rights in Slovenia, I also wonder which institutions are those that need to watch over the realization and protection of human rights. The Constitution and the Constitutional Court are certainly in the first place. In this regard, in particular, I should like to stress that the Social Democrats have requested that, when amending the referendum legislation, it remains explicitly stated that the referendum cannot be signed if a referendum decision interferes with human rights. Then there's an advocate of the principle of equality. As the guardian of human rights, we consider the entire justice system and, last but not least, the commission to petition the National Assembly. When we look at it at European level, it is certainly the Council of Europe, the ECRI – the European Commission against Racism and Intolerance, the European Centre for Human Rights in Vienna, the Commission on Petitions in the European Parliament, and as the crown of all this – the European Court of Human Rights. Let us add to this the Organization of United Nations, international institutions, conventions, human rights instruments. If we were to look at what mechanisms exist to protect human rights, then the number of human rights violations should be reduced. Unfortunately, this world is not nice to people, and violations are often so drastic in the world that they also take human lives. I must not forget the specialized international institutions that classify countries on the scale of respect for human rights. It is also worth mentioning here that every time an index of human development measures in particular non-discrimination in public affairs. If we review all of this, when foreign eyes see us as the most common problems Roma are experiencing, deleted, tenants in denationalized apartments, judicial backlogs, and could also talk about individual cases dealt with in front of institutions. Therefore, it is not irrelevant who Slovenia is delegating to individual European institutions, and the Social Democrats were not satisfied with the selection of representatives and representatives to ECRI and the Vienna Centre for Human Rights. This is because in Slovenia there are competent individuals who have proved themselves in their fight against human rights violations and have not been given the opportunity to operate in international institutions. Among all the problems which Mrs Vlasta Nussdorfer stated in the supplementary explanation, clearly and systematically, I should particularly touch on the issue of the ethics of the public word. Other areas will be touched by my colleagues and colleagues. The fact is that the most important case in 2012 was indeed in the field of social security, judicial and police procedures and constitutional rights, that the Ombudsman’s Office assessed, for 25% of the initiatives under consideration, that they were justified. This is an extremely high percentage of the rationale for initiatives. It's not just a claim that can't be proved with anything. When I mention constitutional rights and in particular the ethics of the public word, the violation of the ethics of the public word appears to be an increased problem for several years in a row. The number of initiatives taken in this area has increased sharply in 2012 – from 353 initiatives to more than 3 times, and the index is 330. In doing so, it should be borne in mind that the number of new initiatives in the field of ethics has increased, as has been said, in all recent years. However, it should also be added at the same time that there is such a significant increase in initiatives at the expense of multiple hearings or complaints at the same time. In particular, it is those matters which the investors of the initiatives consider to be harmful to their feelings and therefore require a response from the state authorities. Let me recall that there is in principle agreement in the National Assembly for the adoption of an ethical code, whose recommendations would also lay down ethical principles that should be followed by Members and Members in their duties as Members. It is much more worrying, however, that the SDS Group, during its recommendations, has proposed that the Ombudsman's recommendation should leave the very part that calls for respect for the ethics of the public word, in particular the holders of political functions. Especially is it to draw attention to hate speech. In doing so, we reiterate that every other position that the addressee cannot accept is not yet an hate speech, and that there has been a great deal of concern in recent times when it comes to adopting different views, which is also affecting the freedom of artistic creation. Unjustifiable interventions into privacy and public publication were mentioned. Here, too, we need to count on everything that can be detected in the media. We attach to the Ombudsman that even in the leadership of RTV Slovenia we should consider the more precise powers of the watcher and listener's rights. The RTV management ensures that this will happen. Now I'm going to touch on just one more question or one more problem, that's the problem of discrimination. Inequality or discrimination has been most noticeable in the past year in reducing pensions under the Public Finance Balance Act. The fact is that the Ombudsman here has demonstrated his response and has submitted an application to the Constitutional Court for a constitutional review, which has also been successful. It has achieved an assessment of the constitutionality of Article 143 of the ZUJF, suspension and absolute priority treatment of this requirement. For years, the Ombudsman has pointed out that the legal framework and institutional mechanisms for eliminating discrimination are not appropriate and in line with the requirements of the European Union acquis. The latest report again recommended the adoption of legal solutions that will be in line with the acquis of the European Union and will ensure an impartial, independent and effective treatment of cases of infringements of the prohibition of discrimination on all grounds and in all areas. Following the Ombudsman's recommendation, it is therefore necessary to establish an independent advocate who would have the power to investigate cases of violations of the prohibition of discrimination and to sanction violations in both the public and private sectors. This work, the private sector, cannot be covered by the Ombudsman. For 2012, the Ombudsman notes not only that the Government and the National Assembly have not taken this recommendation into account but that the situation has worsened. The Office for Equal Opportunities has also been abolished among government offices, under which an advocate of the principle of equality has also acted, which has drowned after reorganisation in the services of the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities. This problem has already been brought to Slovenia's attention in the European Union. In this regard, the Ombudsman also received a letter from the President-in-Office of the European Association of Anti-Discrimination Bodies. On 20 December 2012, this letter was addressed to the Home Ministry, which draws attention to the insufficient operational capacity of the Advocate and to his structural and financial independence. In July this year, the government re-established the Inter-Resort Commission on Human Rights, as the Ombudsman also pointed out. The Minister for Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities, in response to the Member's oral question, replied that the Ministry was preparing a law on counsel. I am aware that we do not have a Member or an individual government to look at this issue in the same way, but the response of this government, despite the poor economic situation, shows, however, that steps have been taken to improve the situation. Speaking of discrimination, the question of the Roma community should certainly be mentioned, namely to invest both sides of the problem, both the Roma community and the non-Roma environment, which requires a different treatment in the allocation of funds for the regulation of irregular Roma settlements and the absorption of European funds. Allow me to finally answer the question that I initially asked myself and saved it for the end — what is the power and effectiveness of the guardian’s work? Many times less than what people expect because the guardian does not have executive powers. However, when we weigh whether the Law on the Ombudsman should be amended after 20 years in the direction of increasing jurisdiction, in this direction, in the Group of Social Democrats, we believe that it is not because it is necessary to ensure the independence of this institution. We believe that over the course of 20 years, it has strengthened its reputation that people are increasingly responding to the Ombudsman, but that luckily, the executive branch of power is also increasingly responsive. Last but not least, the Borut Pahor's government adopted the Ombudsman's report for the first time and the Ombudsman, then Dr Zdenka Čebašek Travnik, presented the report to the government. Last year this tradition was interrupted, but this year Vlasta Nussdorfer Vladi presented the 18th report. Dear Guardian Mrs. Vlasta Nussdorfer, Honourable Deputy Ombudsman, Honourable Minister and Minister, Honourable Secretary of State Mrs. Tina Brecelj and distinguished other members of the Government, honourable Members, honourable Members, everyone's welcome! The protection of human rights in times of economic crisis in all parts of the world is often an unappreciative task. The country's drying financial capacity directly and indirectly affects the enjoyment of civil and political human rights as well as economic, social and cultural rights. The work of the Ombudsman has become increasingly difficult in times of economic crisis, which seems to be increasingly showing off its teeth, and in terms of maintaining human dignity, it has become more and more sensitive. As a result of rising unemployment and resulting greater poverty, people feel humiliated and disgraced. They often find themselves in a hopeless situation, which our legislation certainly contributes to. Let me give you an example that the guardian also draws attention to in the introduction of her report. People are not entitled to free legal aid because they own a few square metres of inherited land from which they have no special benefit but cannot be sold or surrendered. Some for a trifle go beyond the price that free legal aid is available to them, but they are heavily indebted and simply cannot afford legal aid. The Ombudsman's report reflects the real picture of our society today: mobileising in the workplace, suffering and harassment, non-payment of social security contributions, the position of agency and foreign workers, hate speech, the longevity of the proceedings before the courts, discrimination and so on. In doing so, I would particularly like to highlight the situation of the most vulnerable groups of the population, who often cannot fight for their own rights. These are older children, especially those children with special needs. Recently, the case of an aggressive child has drawn attention to the fact that in our country for 20 years we have not been able to establish a section in the form of a crisis accommodation centre where we would properly diagnose and treat children with mental disabilities. These children are hospitalized in closed wards of psychiatric hospitals together with adult patients when it comes to uncontrollable conditions. In 2010, there were five children aged between 10 and 14 and 80 between 15 and 19 years of age. Such conduct is extremely inappropriate and extremely disturbing. By refusing appropriate professional assistance to these children, we deny these children an even small opportunity for normal adult life. I wonder what will have to happen if things actually move. Despite all the warnings, the situation of the elderly in our society continues to deteriorate visibly. Modesty pensions and restrictions on social transfers are increasingly being pressured into poverty and on the edge of society. For some time now, it has been noticeable that the occupation of home for the elderly has fallen. There are several reasons for this, and the most common is the inability to pay for home care services. As a result of the crisis, there are more and more cases of family members taking older ones out of their homes and then unable to provide them with adequate assistance and care at home. Most of the reasons for this are their poor material situation. In these days, older ones often find themselves in a situation where they have to decide whether to eat or warm or to pay the bills or land on the street as homeless people. After years of hard and honest work in the fall of life, they feel justified in being deceived, disappointed, and humiliated. Poor 30% of all pensioners receive a pension of less than 500 euros, which means that all of these pensioners live below the poverty line. At least part of the situation makes it easier for them to own 80 % of the property they live in. The introduction of real estate tax will further place the burden of crisis resolution on their shoulders. The ownership of their homes is priceless for the elderly, since they worked hard for this house in the vast majority and also gave up on many. The property tax, however, will cause these people and their homes to be unable to afford them any longer, thus taking away their last shred of dignity. In the DeSUS Group, we would also like to draw attention again to the growing violence against the elderly and often to disregard their will, as we pointed out in our Group last year. I'd talk more about it, and time won't let me. Many of the Ombudsman’s recommendations are repeated from year to year. Among such judges, for example, is the pedopsychiatric treatment of children and discrimination against the elderly. Clearly, in the areas mentioned above, we do not take the recommendations seriously enough, which means that we are beating the basic human rights of those affected in our country. In recent times, we have often used the crisis and the current state of our country as an excuse. I believe that the crisis must not be an excuse for not taking these problems seriously and not solving them as we should. The DeSUS Group supports the recommendation that all institutions and officials at all levels take into account the recommendations made by the Ombudsman of the Republic of Slovenia in the 18th periodic report of the Ombudsman of the Republic of Slovenia for 2012. Thank you very much.

The instigators felt that the cross was being set on fire as a crime of public incitement to hatred, violence, intolerance and expected action by the state authorities. The Ombudsman confirmed to the initiators and expressed his understanding that this action had caused them to regret religious sentiments and that the police and the prosecution would properly treat and act accordingly. In the response report of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia, however, we read that the police have considered the case as a suspicion of the crime of injuring foreign goods with the provisions of the Penal Code and as a suspicion of the offence of public incitement to hatred, violence or intolerance. On the basis of the notifications collected and the assessment that there is no basis for a criminal complaint, a report was sent to the competent state prosecutor's office. This April, the prosecution informed that there were no signs of a criminal offence. It would probably be interesting to know how many criminal suspicions in this area have been dealt with and how many sanctions have been sanctioned. It is likely that we have not come any further than mentioning the criminal provision in these years of the independent state. The new regime for the exercise of rights from public funds in force from 1 January 2012 and the so-called Svetlik Law have strongly infringed on the rights of the socially weak and have caused them exceptional difficulties in ensuring normal living conditions, especially for young children. An additional serious problem in this area has been the failure of the information system, which has significantly affected the rights of several individuals. Despite the Ministry's assurances that the problems have been resolved, this area needs to be closely monitored. A good week ago, we took note of a case where incomes that were not actually available were taken into account in the decision-making process. The applicants from public funds are usually people who are functionally illiterate, who are already having difficulty exercising the right, not only to complain, because the explanations in the decisions are still unclear to average citizens. It is totally unacceptable that the information system does not allow them another solution, even though the workers of the social work centres themselves have found the error, but have not removed it or have been warned by the parties. This imposes ever-new and new obligations on individuals, but a slight derogation already constitutes a loss of a certain right. The overall risk is identified both on the individual and with concern. Let me point out another example mentioned in the report from this town, namely the area of justice, where it took more than 15 years to complete the abandonment process. The instigator turned to the Ombudsman in the case of the District Court in Maribor, which had not been completed for more than 15 years. There were two other trials pending the completion of this procedure. The Court did not respond to the instigator's lawyer's warning. It is also extremely unacceptable in these circumstances that the Ombudsman’s reply, which justified the reasons for the long procedure, justified the fact that the case had been dealt with by several judges, that the procedure was complex and professionally demanding, that he himself had been absent for a long time and was overloaded by other priority cases due to the staffing difficulties of the Court. I wonder if this is a decent answer to someone whom the National Assembly appoints for our constitutional order for a permanent term of office. Can a permanent term be a silence for a week? We believe that the trial mandate of judges would speed up a number of trials. Unreasonably long procedures and indecisions of judges have a clear impact on the state budget. What is the state of justice and the general state of our rule of law, more than verbally, are the cases in the report. Let me conclude here that we will certainly be alerting Members of the New Slovenia in the next debate, including in relation to past history, to any other case that the Ombudsman's report does not contain. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u21	Thank you, too. Ms Eva Irgl will present the position of the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian Democratic Party.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u22	Thank you so much for saying that. Honourable Guardian of Human Rights with a Deputy, Honourable Member, Honourable Members! As has already been said, we have before us the 18th regular annual report of the Ombudsman for 2012. As already explained in the introduction, the Ombudsman, Mrs Vlasta Nussdorfer, is this time acting as the guardian for the first time in the role of presenting a report, which, by the way, was produced under the auspices of the former Ombudsman, Mrs Zdenke Cebašek Travnik. However, before I focus on the very content of the report, let me explain that it is the role of the Ombudsman that is extremely important for the good functioning of the rule of law and, above all, the just state and society. It is the Ombudsman who is called upon to protect human rights and fundamental freedoms in relation to state authorities, local authorities and public authorities. The Ombudsman is the first person and authority in the country to take a constitutional decision in respect of respect for human rights. It must decide, on the basis of declarations and resolutions on human rights, on the basis of international acts, conventions, laws, and, by no means, on such sensitive issues as are most likely to affect human integrity and dignity, to decide or to judge politically. When he begins to judge his observations about human rights violations politically and not according to the principles of human rights protection, he should leave his position to someone else. In the Slovenian Democratic Party, we are of the view that the Ombudsman must also take a clear stand for current events if he realizes that they have been tested for human rights in any way. Why am I saying this? In the past, we have repeatedly observed that, even when the Ombudsmans were invited by various institutions, including the National Assembly, to identify certain issues of human rights violations, they have not done so. In our opinion, the Ombudsman must raise his voice when the rule of law is under test. The Ombudsman must raise his voice when the social state is under test. Today, there are many problems that stem from the social state's test. The Ombudsman must raise his voice when the right state is under test. Unfortunately, in the past, the Ombudsman did not always act in accordance with these principles, but all too often politically. The Ombudsman's annual report presents an important document on respect for and protection of human rights. The Ombudsman, Mrs Vlasta Nussdorfer, notes in her introduction that in 25% of the initiatives coming to their title are assessed as justified. They add that this is a large proportion, given that the Ombudsman is only an additional means of protecting human rights and freedoms. The Ombudsman’s report each year describes the problems that individuals face when they feel they have been violated by human rights. They address the Ombudsman's office when they face the fact that the institutions of the State do not listen to them as much as they would like to. This problem is that certain institutions do not do their job well enough, we face, for example, the Commission for Petitions and for human rights and equal opportunities, often after several hours, either we do not get certain answers or we get them in the late time frame. This problem is also faced by Members and Members, when we ask, say, questions to ministers. Some have waited four months for their answers. I understand you can't answer all the questions right away, but there are some deadlines that need to be followed. The non-response of some authorities is therefore worrying. We still have a slow response from inspection services, which the Ombudsman reminds us of year after year, even in the 2012 report. The answers of inspection services are often selfless and often contradictory. The duration of the proceedings and the failure to respond to the incentives shall be repeated. This raises the issue of prioritisation in the work of inspection services. Therefore, in this report, the Ombudsman considers that the criteria for determining the order of treatment should be laid down in the regulation, since transparency would only be ensured and confidence in the work of inspection services would be increased. We welcome this in the Slovenian Democratic Party, and we see this recommendation as good. The Ombudsman's report also proposes certain solutions that the Government and other institutions will consider each year, and we are also talking about these recommendations in the National Assembly. At the same time, the Ombudsman warns that many times, with their warnings, they run into deaf ears and that some of their recommendations are repeated year after year. We also observe in the Slovenian Democratic Party that the Ombudsman repeats several recommendations, even though they have already been implemented in legislation or in some other way. For this very reason, we have repeatedly proposed in the Slovenian Democratic Party a package of our recommendations to the Ombudsman's annual report. Every year, the Ombudsman's report also shows us at least a partial picture of how patient society we are, how much tolerance we actually go to other thinkers, how much we really give to the protection and respect of human rights, and, above all, what our relationship as a society, as individuals, is to the weakest, most vulnerable groups. Through the Ombudsman's reports, we can see that people are in ever-increasing difficulties, especially social ones, especially today when the country is in a severe financial and economic crisis, as well as a crisis of values. It is therefore incomprehensible that the government of Alenka Bratušek, by its actions, is further interfering with and destroying the social state. As a result, it is even more incomprehensible that those who have barely survived are pushing to the brink of survival, including by enforcing a property tax, by increasing the prices of vineyards, by extra burdening families with multiple children in particular, to increase the prices of transport of children, and today citizens and citizens will have to pay more than one credit due to the government's decision to go into uncontrolled liquidation of two private banks. On the other hand, the government of Alenka Bratusek retains the privileges of some individuals through extremely high pensions. In Slovenia, there are many people who have been violated by human rights, who are in severe distress and need help, who have been warning about injustices for years and years, such as victims after World War II or individuals who have been alerting for many years to anomalies in the judicial system – more so later – or people who have been violated by the constitutional right to a healthy environment for years. Here we're talking, say, about noise, smell, pollution with dangerous PM10 particles. The Ombudsman notes that if we look only at the noise, there is an increasing number of disruptive noise initiatives every year. Problems arise because noise rules are only determined by measurements, and noise measurements are often carried out when there is no overrun. When we talk about smell from different sources, from composters, cleaning appliances, pig farms, turkey farms – I have also repeatedly warned about these things. Some years ago, even on the Commission for Petitions and for human rights and equal opportunities, we had a meeting on this subject and discussed this item on the agenda. This is also a common reason for initiatives and actions. This is particularly a reminder of the need for the adoption of a smell-emitting regulation into the environment, as no regulation governs this, which means that control is not possible as well. Problems also arise in the area of measurement of discharges, where regulations are established, but discharge measurements should be carried out by authorities who are totally independent of operators. The Ombudsman also notes in his report the issue of pollution with dangerous PM10 particles, particularly in the Mežiški valley, the Celje Valley and Zasavje. Improving air quality due to excess particles requires the measures already required by the European Commission, but has not yet been implemented in our space. Even the most recent studies, if we look at it, show that 90% of the European Union's citizens are exposed to the most powerful air pollutants, at levels that are highly harmful to health. The report, adopted by the European Environment Agency and published this week, shows that the two main pollutants are air, ozone and particulate matter, still cause respiratory problems and cause cardiovascular diseases and shorten life. New research also shows that already lower concentrations of air pollutants affect health. According to the 2011 Eurostat European Statistical Office, Slovenia has been contaminated with particulate matter at 10th in the European Union and ozone pollution at 3rd in the EU. All the information I have just said shows that the right to a healthy living environment at this time is particularly on trial and requires greater concern from all of us that the space in which we live is more secure, even due to future generations. The next topic I want to touch, draw attention to, is the area of justice. Also in the Ombudsman's 18th annual report, this time, the Ombudsman notes that the rule of law is on a major test, in particular because of the violation of the right to an effective remedy and the trial within a reasonable time. The Ombudsman also notes that the State should provide more quality and wider available free legal aid, which must be available to customers in a timely manner. Let me explain that the Members of the Slovenian Democratic Party, and probably others, are returning to us individuals who are not entitled to free legal aid, and this, as well as the Ombudsman, is described in her report, only because for a slightity they are exceeding the price to which free legal aid is still available, but because of the situation in which this country is located and because of personal hardship, even because they may be heavily indebted, they cannot afford legal aid. However, the government of Alenka Bratušek still raises the charges of court and further restricts access to justice and judicial protection for individuals. In the field of judicial proceedings, the Ombudsman dealt with 460 cases this year. Of these, they were most closely linked to civil and criminal procedures. Members of the Slovenian Democratic Party receive various initiatives from individuals due to dissatisfaction with the functioning of the courts, the longevity of the judicial proceedings and doubts about the impartiality of the judges. Many initiatives also refer to the expectations of the initiators, following a substantive assessment by the Commission on Petitions and for human rights and equal opportunities. In this regard, we need to explain to the initiator several times that we do not have the power to assess the regularity and legality of the judicial decisions taken and their content, and therefore explain to them what legal remedies are available to them. However, while we must not interfere with the judiciary as the legislative branch of power, I would like to explain that more and more people are reporting to us that they have been or are victims of judicial errors. Several meetings referred to the topic of judicial abnormalities, and I have explained several times that in the Slovenian Democratic Party we have been drawing attention to the inefficiencies of the judicial system for many years to remind us of the longevity of bankruptcy and other proceedings. For many years, we have been opening up questions about the duration of the judicial mandate. What is most likely the most crucial at this time – we also open up questions about taking responsibility in the judicial and prosecutorial categories. We also point to strong solidarity when individuals in the judiciary protect each other, and this is usually why no one is responsible for mistakes. Let's just see a picture of the sanctions imposed on judges in Slovenia compared to some other European countries. We've said this a number of times, both from this town and from the bench. Comrade Gorenak once had a thorough analysis of the state of justice. But let me repeat some information again, because it's extremely important. The data show that Slovenia has three times as many judges and prosecutors as some other members of the European Union per 100,000 inhabitants, but that their effectiveness is significantly lower and there is little responsibility for errors in proceedings. In Italy, for example, 51 sanctions were imposed on judges in 2006 and 25 in Germany and only one sanction in Slovenia in the same period. That probably tells us something. That probably doesn't mean the judges are infallible. However, it appears that they do not accept responsibility or protect one another after a mistake has occurred. Here, allow me to remind myself, for example, of Foykar. I've mentioned it many times, and I'll probably do it again. This is a very tragic story, or, thank God, with a happy ending, but a story that confirms that the rule of law does not work as far as we should. Above all, this story confirms that no one answers due to procedural and substantive errors in the process. I have met several times, both as a Member of Parliament and as President of the Commission on Human Rights, with Mr Fojkar, who entrusted me with his calvary and wanted us to help him years ago. I then drew his attention to all the relevant national authorities, including the Ombudsman. I must say that she has advocated this case, as well as the Ministry of Justice and the Supreme Public Prosecutor's Office. Mr Fojkar was stigmatized because of the long-term criminal procedure and his medical condition was very poor because of all these long-term procedures. He's still feeling the consequences today. It was a controversial way of working as a district attorney, who, in the light of the excusable judgment, also referred to by the district judge. But nothing happened to the D.A. in this case. She's never been responsible for any mistakes she's made. Here I would like to thank once again my fellow Member, Mr Gorenak, on behalf of the State, Mr Fojkar, for his 11-year personal calvary. Let us take another example, which is related, for example, to Mrs Franje Šilich. You know this case very well, at least I hope, because it has been in the public several times before, but it may need further clarifications and clarifications. The case of Mrs Franje Šilih is such that the proceedings in the courts concerning her son's death have been pending for 21 years. In 1993, a medical error occurred. Despite the accuracy of the investigation and the suspicions raised, the prosecution did not want to carry out or take over. I hope that Mrs Zdenki Cerar, the late Attorney-General of the State, is not going to blame me if I tell you what has happened on one of the multiple visits at this time. She said it wasn't such a climate that we could fight medical errors. As a result, they took over the case by themselves as a subsidiary prosecutor with their husband and collected sufficient evidence to make the indictment legal with the 1999 decision. That was the first time, but not the last mistake. The defendant's charge was not served. Why? Most likely because complaints were made at the time by the accused physicians and the hospital, and ultimately the case was dismissed as unfounded and without sufficient evidence. Two years after his death, Shilih’s spouses also filed a compensation action. Everything was rejected. But later they decided to sue the country of Slovenia and succeeded in the European Court of Human Rights for violating the Convention, namely in the article on the right to life and because Slovenia does not have an institution to identify medical errors. In Slovenia they also succeeded in the Supreme Court of Justice, Constitutional Court, and the case was returned to the Court of First Instance, the District Court in Maribor, where Shilih's spouses went this third time, not to mention that they were not allowed to interview an expert. That is why we can rightly ask ourselves in such stories whether this seems to you to be a just and legal state. It is an excruciating injustice that has been found by the European Court of Human Rights, and we still have procedures, because it is more than obvious that certain people are being protected, perhaps even in some way in the wake of health-justice. As a result of such judicial conduct, people get sick, are financially bankrupt, and, above all, because of the ever-increasing new and long-lasting procedures, they cannot find inner peace so that they can finally, despite the pain that remains forever, breathe more freely and without burden. When we talk about justice, we can't get past the prosecution of economic crime. I already told the Commission on Petitions and Human Rights and Equal Opportunities, when we discussed the annual report, that economic crime is also a major violation of human rights in our country. Let me mention here fake bankruptcys, damage to creditors, business fraud, fraud in obtaining loans or benefits, tax evasion, misrepresentation of tax administrations, money laundering, suspicious transactions and so on. All of these crimes have resulted in many human rights violations committed against employees by direct or direct owners of companies, many of which have come up in a suspicious manner. At this point in the Slovenian Democratic Party, we expect the Ombudsman to act more on his own initiative and not only on the basis of an application or initiative, in the face of such grave human rights violations, which appear particularly in the abuse of workers. We must be aware that individuals are afraid to speak out out of fear of possible, say, retaliatory measures. However, it is the Ombudsman who has the opportunity, under Article 26 of the Law on Human Rights, to initiate proceedings on his own initiative and not only by waiting for applications and initiatives from individuals affected. At this point in time, I am moving from justice to social affairs, in particular to two key issues of this time, in which we are, in general, in our country – unemployment and the issue of poverty. Much has already been said about this, especially in the position of the Slovenian People's Party, but allow me to say something about it myself. Unemployment has skyrocketed ever since the beginning of 2009. As a result of the consequences of corporate bankruptcy, more and more people are in such a difficult social situation and personal distress that they simply no longer see the way out of the vicious circle. So many times they face despair, discouragement, pessimism, which can unfortunately lead to the worst consequences when they no longer see the meaning of life. Unemployment resulting from financial distress, loneliness, isolation — all of this has become a constant in our society. In particular, people with disabilities, long-term unemployed, over 55 years of age, and more difficult to deal with are exposed to this. It is precisely these categories that the new Social Entrepreneurship Act 2011 is aimed at, unfortunately in practice, not yet fully revived. Today only 14 social enterprises are registered in Slovenia, while the social enterprise sector in Europe is growing and represents 10% of all European companies and employs more than 11 million people. That is why it would also be necessary for us to revive social entrepreneurship as soon as possible, which, as we see in Europe, also has an important impact on the state of employment. Unfortunately, the number of unemployed is increasing from day to day, causing a negative atmosphere in both local and national environments. The statistics collected by the Slovenian Employment Office show that the unemployment rate increased to 13.4% by the year 2008 by an average of 6,7%. Speaking of unemployment, we can't get past poverty. There are many of you in Slovenia from year to year. The Ombudsman, Mrs Vlasta Nussdorfer, points out that she faces people, everywhere and often faces poverty and social pressures in her field. This highlights the situation of children from poor families who, according to her belief, should not share the fate of their parents. The Ombudsman's findings show that legislation unfortunately often fails in practice when people face poverty and are powerless. In doing so, she also warned from civil society that the efforts of NGOs, which contribute significantly to and put an end to the consequences of the crisis, must not be kept quiet to the State in failing to carry out their task in the fight against poverty. Psychologists warn that children living in poverty or on the brink of poverty have a very poor perspective in terms of developmental psychology. As they said in UNICEF Slovenia a long time ago – I quote: "We are at the point when we can lose an entire generation of children to poverty." It should be borne in mind that the consequences of bad decisions will not only be felt during the current crisis period but only much later. Mrs Anita Ogulin – we all know her as a fighter to help the poor, the poorest. The secretary of the Association of Youth Friends Ljubljana Moste–Polje points out that in Slovenia poverty has become a problem number 1. According to data from the Statistical Office of the Republic of Slovenia, 392 thousand people or 14% were excluded from the risk of poverty or social exclusion last year. This means that their income was lower than €606 per person per month. To illustrate more plasticly: a 4-member family with two adult children and two children under 14 years of age had to have at least €1273 of net income available per month, and a 2-member household without children had to have at least €909 per month to rise above the poverty threshold. Even Slovenian humanitarian organisations, and here they are all thanking, are pointing out the great problem of poverty. A congress was held in October to highlight the problems of poverty and social exclusion in Slovenia and, at the same time, the increasingly indispensable role of humanitarian organisations in alleviating these pressures and problems. The purpose of the Congress was to draw attention to social exclusion, which is also a result of poverty, and to say that the research data carried out by the Forum last year show that 744 000 people in Slovenia needed the assistance of humanitarian organisations at least once last year. Now I would briefly touch on two more topics, namely, the topics that appear within the Ombudsman's report not only in this report, but practically all the years since I've been accompanying it – this is a matter of sensational reporting. The Ombudsman warns that the media are giving the advantage of sensationalism too many times. It also warns that it is unacceptable to disclose the identity of children in the media. What is particularly worrying is the fact that such violations are reported in sensationalism when family tragedies are reported, while revealing the identity and privacy of children, despite warning, are still taking place. With regard to the legislation on the protection of children in the media, it should also be said that thank God the Law on the Prevention of Domestic Violence was passed years ago, and a specific article regulates this particular issue. The victims or perpetrators of violence shall not be subject to publicly transmitted data on the basis of which the victim or her family could be identified. Data may only be transmitted with the consent of a full-year victim. This law imposes a responsibility on parents, foster parents, foster parents or foster parents to protect children from exposure to the public in the context of the implementation of their care. As a criminal offence, however, the Penal Code defines the publication of personal data of a child who is a participant in a judicial, administrative or any other procedure or the publication of other information on the basis of which his or her identity could be identified. The penalty is a fine or a sentence of up to three years. In her report, the Ombudsman also draws attention to mobbing or suffering in the workplace. There's more than that. We also receive such complaints against the Commission for petitions and for human rights and equal opportunities. We have also dealt with this subject several times, including with the Committee on Labour, the Family, Social Affairs and Disabled Persons, but there is something essential to say about the fact that the Penal Code was adopted at the time of the first Janša Government, which defined the crime of the mobbing under Article 197, which is punishable by imprisonment for up to two years. Article 6 of the Labour Relations Act also prohibits sexual and other harassment and torture in the workplace. The Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia also specifies that everyone has the right to personal dignity and security, which means that state authorities and all individuals must respect the right to personal dignity and security. The consequences of the mobbinge are also well aware of the European Union, which warns that one third of all working days are lost due to disease and absence, which are unfortunately all too often due to the mobbinga. The costs incurred by this mannequin go to thousands of billions of euros. This is enough information to suggest that we need to discuss this further so that we may at some point be able to hold another meeting on this subject with the Ombudsman, as it is a phenomenon within the working environment that we must take force seriously. We could also talk about such and other violations of human rights and fundamental freedoms, which the Ombudsman also points out within the Ombudsman's 2012 report, but some topics will be opened by my fellow Members as well as by myself. Otherwise, the Slovenian Democratic Party's Group will support the report and the Ombudsman's recommendation. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u34	Thank you very much, Madam Vice President. A respected choir! I support this report. It's very extensive. I am speaking only on one segment of this report. In view of the petition we have received from the Group of Social Democrats, I will only touch on one problem in the area of housing. These are the problems that residents have in multi-housing buildings with managers. In general, I can say that the Ombudsman is not satisfied with the situation in the area of housing relations because there is no progress. The recommendations from year to year remain unfulfilled. The last government response report for 2011 also shows that housing matters are properly regulated, that legislation is appropriate and that there is no need for change. This was followed by the National Assembly, which in that area did not accept any recommendation from the Ombudsman to improve the situation. However, already in the 2011 report, the Ombudsman pointed out the need for the adoption of the National Housing Programme for 2012-2021, which is still in preparation. Interestingly, only 44 initiatives are being addressed by the Ombudsman in 2012 in the area of housing, which is significantly less than a year ago when there were 93 such initiatives. According to the Ombudsman, such a decline can also be linked to people losing confidence in the country and those institutions that can help them. In the 2012 report, there are no issues relating to governance issues in multi-housing buildings and problems faced by tenants in relation to managers. This does not mean that these problems do not exist in the country. This is how representatives of residents from Bežigrad, Mesarska, Novi Poljan and two neighbours from Šiška in Ljubljana addressed us. Since they have serious problems with operators, they have presented an initiative to amend the current housing law, which would make it easier to replace the manager in multi-housing buildings. Following the current arrangements, the appointment of the manager during the regular management business shall be made. This means that such decisions are taken by the owners of a multi-entity building whose ownership shares on this property together exceed 50 %. As a result, the replacement of the manager is extremely difficult in multi-housing buildings, where there is a large number of co-owners. Instigators propose to set the building’s managers’ term of office for 4 to 5 years, and for a new mandate, the manager should make an effort and obtain more than 50% of the consent for the building’s co-owners — vice versa — as is the case today. Such arrangements would force the manager to become a more competent manager in order to carry out his work more transparently and more cost-effectively. What problems do residents face? Thus, for example, one of the co-management or neighbourhood management contracts was signed between the builders of the neighbourhoods themselves before the apartments were even sold to the new owners. Such contracts are also signed for 8, 10 years in advance, so that the owners did not actually have and have no impact on the selection of the governor of the neighborhood. In doing so, they drew attention to the possible commissions of managers by the operators themselves, such as for certain repairs, insurance companies and the like. It's an enormous amount of money that managers collect from their tenants. The signatories to this petition pointed out, however, that there is a suspicion of an irrational and unintended use of the funds they are paying to reserve funds. Thus, in one case, residents collected up to 80% of signatures to replace the administrator and still have not reached a replacement because there is a constant complication and delaying them. Also on the government portal I propose to the government it is possible to track similar initiatives. In particular, the joint denominator of these initiatives is the call for more effective protection of minority co-owners in multi-housing buildings, which are practically not protected in cases where managers do not either perform their work or do not perform it conscientiously and economically, as provided by legislation. I propose to the Ombudsman that he continue his work to address the problems of tenants in multi-housing buildings. Thank you very much.
