As for single-parent families, we are introducing an increase in the allowance for single-parent families per child for cash social assistance from 26.52 euros to 53 euros. We introduce only part of the child allowance in other rights under the Law on the exercise of rights from public resources, for example in the case of kindergarten subsidies. The total child allowance is now taken into account as an additive and, as proposed, the allowance for single parent families and 20% of the first child allowance in the first class will not be taken into account. If I move on to the area of older recipients of social transfers. In persons who are permanently unemployed, permanently unemployed or unemployed, women over 63 years of age or men over 65 years of age, or in families with such a person, we introduce that the money of a single person up to EUR 2 000 500 is not taken into account as assets. Now this price is 795.66 euros, or up to EUR 3 000 500 for the family, and I will no longer mention it. Censuses are increasing, which I believe is an absolutely positive measure in these times of crisis for those who are most at risk. Many individuals have decided to waive the right to a protective allowance in order to record the prohibition on the disposal and loading of property. In this regard, we introduce that the limitation on the inheritance of the property of the person receiving the protective allowance may be limited only to the amount of two thirds of the value of the aid received. We introduce access to the disability committees of the STIZ to obtain an opinion on permanent incapacity for work and consequently entitlement to a protective allowance. Even in the area of families with children in education, the situation is improving. State scholarships for underage students are being introduced, but we also maintain a higher amount of child allowance for underage students who are not eligible for a state grant. There are also subsidised snacks under certain conditions. In the area of large families, we introduce that savings of three minimum family incomes are no longer taken into account as assets in determining eligibility for financial social assistance. This is where we increase censorship. We introduce only part of the child allowance in other rights under the Law on the exercise of rights from public resources. Now, let's say, income takes into account the whole children's allowance. We are improving the conditions for obtaining exceptional financial social assistance. For example, we are instituting the acquisition of exceptional financial social assistance for beneficiaries of a protective allowance, which, for example, may be newly eligible for this aid for emergency roof repair, heating and post payment. Currently, they can only benefit from this aid for the payment of costs incurred by natural disasters, floods and so on. We introduce a change in the consideration of income in the child allowance, the State grant and the reduced payment of kindergarten for persons receiving cash social assistance. Here, too, I would like to point out that the warning has been taken into account that income will no longer be taken into account one or two years ago, but that current income will be taken into account and thus the current situation of the family in a crisis situation. We are introducing a more favourable consideration of informal incomes, where student work is also considered. We are introducing a free mindset for monetary social assistance and a protective allowance. We introduce a more favourable account of the change in periodic incomes, extend the range of beneficiaries to financial social assistance and a protective allowance by increasing the amount of assets that are not taken into account for the acquisition of monetary social assistance or a security allowance. The payment of social security services and the family assistant should be subject to new income only in the last three months. A number of positive measures will be taken by the government of Alenka Bratušek, and I believe that any indication that this government is taking action into the social state and destroying the social state is unfounded. I believe that mistakes have been made in the past and, I say, if we maintain a sense of empathy, we will correct the mistakes that have been made, even though the crisis is – at least so much so that we will not interfere with those really minimum standards of the most socially sensitive people. ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u98	Thank you very much, Madam Vice President. Colleagues and colleagues, good-bye! The Committee on Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Disabled Persons has considered the proposal for a law amending the Law on Social Protection, submitted by the Group of Members and Members to the National Assembly for consideration and adoption pursuant to Article 88 of the Constitution and Article 19 of the Law on Members, with the first-signed Mr Kurnjek. Representatives of the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities, representative of the Legislative and Legal Service, representative of the State Council and representatives of the public concerned, participated in the work of the Committee. The Committee was given an opinion by the Legislative and Legal Service, which examined the draft law in terms of its compliance with the Constitution, the legal system and the legislative technical aspect and had no comments on it. The Committee was informed of the opinion of the Government, which does not support the proposal. The Commission of the State Council for Social Protection, Labour, Health and Disabled Persons in its opinion also did not express support for the law. During the course of business, qualified applicants have not tabled any amendments. The representative of the proponents said in the introductory explanation that they had put forward this proposal in good faith to remove another unnecessary obstacle to the economy by means of amendments. The amendment proposed removes the regime that extends the pay system of the public sector to private operators as well. In Article 46 of the Act amending the Law on Social Protection, which refers to the very content of the tender for the award of a concession for the performance of a public service, they would delete the eighth indent of the first paragraph. This talks about the very content of the invitation to tender, which should also include the indication that the labour relations of employees are regulated in accordance with collective agreements, laws and other acts applicable to employees in public institutions in the field of social protection. Following the introduction of the law, they consulted on the basis of all the information and professional arguments obtained and decided not to insist on the law. As proponents, they listened to all the professional arguments and found that, despite the fact that they would like to relieve the economy slightly, the proposal also poses certain risks that are too great at this point to insist on the proposal. In view of the initial explanation by the representative of the applicants, the Committee adopted the conclusion that the draft Act amending the Law on Social Protection was not supported or not suitable for further consideration. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u99	Thank you very much. I also give the floor to the representative of the drafters of the bill to Mr Branko Kurnjek for an introductory presentation of the position on the proposal of the home working body.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u100	Thank you very much for your words, Madam Vice President. Dear Members! In my opinion as a proposaler, I will also state the position of our parliamentary group at the same time, because we were the only proposers for amending this law. In Article 46 of the Act, which deals with the content of the invitation to tender for the award of a public service concession, the proposal to amend the law introduced in the Civil Service Charter would delete the eighth indent of the first paragraph. The eighth indent provides guidance that the content of the invitation to tender should also include the indication that the employment relationships of employees are regulated in accordance with collective agreements, laws and other acts applicable to employees in public institutions in the field of social protection. In short, this means that a proposal to amend the law would remove the regime that extends the pay system of the public sector to private operators as well. We have tabled a proposal to amend the law in the parliamentary group with good intentions to remove another unnecessary obstacle to the economy and to follow the principle of free economic initiative set out in Article 74 of the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia. Following the amendment of the law, we have obtained reservations from the public concerned and have pointed out in particular that the law could put additional pressure on workers with a possible reduction in wages. Let me say that our purpose was precisely reversed because the wage system in the public sector is rigid. This amendment of the law should also have a strong impact on the non-compliance with standards and standards. I would like to make it clear that we have been skeptical about this in the Civil Gazette, because the prescribed standards and regulations are separate from the pay system, but we have decided not to insist in amending the law that we do not really intervene in the categories mentioned above. We were also aware or better of the fact that the adoption of such a law could have a domino effect, say, in education, in health care and elsewhere. Unfortunately, our society is not ready for this. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u102	Thank you very much for saying so, Madam Vice President. Colleagues and colleagues! Members of the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian People's Party do not support this bill and support the conclusion that the law is not suitable for further consideration. We did the same at a meeting of the parental committee. We oppose the law in the parliamentary group because of the principle of equality of all business entities operating in the public social protection network. Concessionists become part of a public network by obtaining concessions. Therefore, they are not private persons, as is mistakenly thought, and therefore must respect the conditions and regulations governing the operation of the public social network, including the regulation of labour relations in accordance with collective agreements and other acts applicable to employees in the public network. They must therefore work under the same conditions, otherwise they could even talk about unfair competition. The provisions under collective agreements, the compulsory payment of wages and the fulfilment of workers' rights do not in any way constitute a restriction on the freedom of business or the restriction of an economic initiative, but rather constitute an equal respect for the rights of all employees performing such services. It is also possible to agree with workers' representatives that all in the same network must be subject to the same standards and standards and labour costs. We welcome the decision of the applicants to take account of the professional arguments and to propose on the committee themselves the rejection of the bill. That is why we will also vote in the SLS Group in favour of the Committee's decision that the law is not suitable for further consideration.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u103	Thank you very much. On behalf of the parliamentary group of the Democratic Party of Pensioners of Slovenia, Mrs Marjana Kotnik Popotat will present the position.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u104	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. After the drafter of this law had thus withdrawn from the proposal of the law, the Labour Authority concluded that the proposed Act amending the Law on Social Protection was inappropriate for further consideration. Therefore, the debate on the content of the law itself is in fact irrelevant today, as the applicant no longer shows interest in adopting this law. In the DeSUS Group, we cannot simply go beyond the content of this bill, since it is very important for the functioning of social institutions, especially institutions in the field of home operations for older citizens. The Parliamentary Group of the Civil Code has proposed that collective agreements, laws and other acts applicable to employees in public institutions in the field of social protection should no longer apply to employees employed in concessionaires providing such services. When it comes to the implementation of activities that fall under the auspices of the public network of health and social services, i.e. when it comes to the provision of services in the public interest and are necessary and necessary for the life, health and residence of people dependent on foreign assistance, it is important that these services are provided in the same scope and quality, regardless of whether they are involved in a public institution or a private concessionaire. It is therefore important that employees of both types of employers are treated in the same way as they are practically the same type of work. If the owners of private homes for the elderly with a concession no longer have to pay the employees under collective contracts for health and social protection, only the minimum wage could be paid to the workers. Not only would this at the expense of employees constitute unfair competition for public institutions, since the dumped prices would allow older people to take over from public homes, it would also be very likely that the quality of services would fall. In any event, it is essential that employees of this activity are treated equally, regardless of whether they work in a public institution or in a concessionaire, because they do the same type of work. Working with the elderly in a nursing home with a concession is just as difficult as working in a public nursing home. I think it is right that the applicant has assessed that the adoption of the proposed amendment would have unacceptable consequences for the quality of the services provided. In the DeSUS Group, we will vote in favour of the Committee's decision that the law is inappropriate for further consideration. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u134	Thank you very much. Land debt is created on the basis of a unilateral legal transaction in the form of a notary record by entering into the land book and by issuing a land letter. It can only be established by the owner of the property as well as by a mortgage creditor, e.g. a bank that converts, in agreement with the owner of the encumbered property, its mortgage into one or more land debts. Land debt is disproportionate, which means that it is independent of any claim or legal business. It's moving along with the land letter. Since the introduction of this Institute, and most intensively since 2011, the public has been informed not so much about the alleged utility benefits of this Institute, but about the increasing abuse or use that does not serve its purpose, which has led to the introduction of the Institute into the Slovenian legal system at all. A surprising number of cases can be observed in the media where individuals, including some influential economic operators, in order to prevent or make it difficult for a State or other creditors to enforce their properties, the latter have been burdened with allegedly fictitious land debts with values that have also more than several times exceeded the value of the fixed property and with extremely long maturitys of the land debt obligation. On this basis, it can reasonably be concluded that there are so-called fraudulistic frauds, fraudulent land debts. They are characterised by the fact that the owner of the property, which is threatened with enforcement for the settlement of his debts or even the confiscation of property, by the establishment of the land debt, mobilises his economic value in a land letter transferred by presentation to the related person. The holder of the land letter is not the real owner or beneficiary of the land letter, but is only a strawed man, a fiduciary who, in his own name, exercises rights from land debt in the account of the owner of the encumbered property. The purpose of such a manoeuvre is to land at least the proceeds from the sale of the property in the hands of the owner or founder of the land debt and not with the State or with other creditors. In the Slovenian legal space, the Institute of Land Debt in fair business practice did not apply at all. Since the establishment of this Institute in 2003, there were an average of 50, 60 registered land debts in the Land Book at the annual level in 2011. According to data from the Supreme Court of the Republic of Slovenia, up to the declining increase in registered land debts in 2013 there is a marked increase in enrolments, namely the first half of 2012 326, only in half years, in 2012 as much as 730 and in 2013 up to about 3 000. In view of the fact that the Association of Banks of Slovenia has provided information that its members, i.e. banks, are not serving land debt to cover their claims or that they are primarily using the maximum mortgage for this purpose and that the share of the land debt in the total quota is only good, we find that this institution does not serve its purpose but serves something else. Some experts estimate that there are at least three quarters of fictitious land debts in the Republic of Slovenia. It follows from the above that it is imperative that this moment be the only good decision that this institution, which has never come to life and which has turned into an ordinary fraud and abuse of rights, should simply be abolished. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u157	Thank you very much, Mr Grimes. The following is a presentation of the views of the groups of Members. The first is Mrs Ludmila Novak on behalf of the New Slovenia Group.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u158	Let me begin by explaining that the Members of the New Slovenia Group will support the amendments and additions to the Notariat Act, which we are considering today, with some comments which will be presented below. The solutions proposed provide equal access to notary occupation for all citizens of the European Union, the Swiss Confederation, the European Economic Area and the OECD. The opportunities of these citizens with the citizens of the Republic of Slovenia for access to the profession of notary are being levelled. That amendment to the Notariat Act means compliance with the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union, which concluded that the notary profession is not related to the exercise of public authority within the meaning of Article 45(1) of the EU Treaty or Article 51 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the EU. In fact, the interiors generally carry out a certification activity which is not directly related to the exercise of power. Therefore, restricting access to the Notary profession on the basis of the condition of nationality is not justified. In New Slovenia, we are not inclined to constantly amend legislation, as this makes its implementation difficult. However, if interventions into marriage are really necessary, one novel should cover all necessary changes. The Minister of Justice announced legislative changes in the area of notariat a year ago and specifically highlighted the abolition of the numerus Clausus system. This is a restriction on the number of Notary cities. At that time, the minister was still a member of the previous government, and we welcomed this proposal. That is why in New Slovenia we expected that this novelty will be covered by this very novelty, which we believe is really necessary and necessary. Many European countries do not have a limited number of notary cities, and this change would bring them closer to the regulation of comparable Member States of the European Union. Anyone who meets the legal conditions for appointment to a notary place may become notary. More importantly, among notaries we would create the competition needed to ensure higher quality of services and also lower prices. According to New Slovenia, notary services in Slovenia are essentially expensive and overestimated. Although the prices of some of their services have already fallen. In addition, notaries shall acquire additional powers. The latest such news is the notary sale of immovable property secured by mortgage on the basis of a directly enforceable notary record. These procedures will be difficult, extensive and costly. Many notaries are already overloaded. Maintaining a limited number of notaries is contrary to the objectives pursued by the Government with the amendment of the Financial Insurance Act, which is shorter procedures. In Novi Slovenija we expect amendments and additions to the Notariat Act in the direction announced by the Minister before to be introduced into the parliamentary procedure.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u160	Thank you very much for your words, Madam Vice President. Dear Madam Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues! In the Italian and Hungarian Group of the National Communities, we regret that the drafter of the law and the majority in the home labour body did not recognize the importance of our amendment to the open article of the law amending the Notariat Act. Since there have been concrete problems in this area in the near past and we have also discussed the possibility of the above-mentioned law-building in the past, we have legitimately tabled an amendment to the good faith that this legal-form gap is also being cut. The amendment was made because there is only one sentence, so that in Article 7, paragraph 8, the text is upgraded so that in the area where the Italian or Hungarian national communities live, the two languages are also spoken in addition to the Slovenian language. As a justification for the eligibility of our amendment, I should like to point out that the public and official use of the Italian or Hungarian language in the national mixed area is primarily laid down in Article 11 of the Constitution. This right can, however, be linked to Articles 64, 5 and 62 of the Constitution and can also be found in the act of independence by law on the ratification of the European Charter on Regional or Minority Languages and the Framework Convention for the Protection of Minorities, in the special statute of the London Agreement and in other legal acts on the use of language. Since notariat is a public service, which also clearly provides for Article 1 of the Notariat Act, this condition should also apply to notaries. Due to the sensitivity of some personal and other cases in the context of the Notary Service, handling matters by translation can be an exceptional case, and by no means a rule, as some have explained. It should not be questionable that notaries for the occupation of employment in national mixed areas should also meet the conditions of knowledge of Italian or Hungarian. The argument that there could be a situation where we do not have a suitable candidate, who also masters the language of indigenous national communities, should not, in our opinion, be a reason to reduce the constitutional level of the rights of indigenous national communities in the area of legislation. Unfortunately, the lack of personnel, which we note, is probably not the fault of an individual or of the national community, so these interpretations do not materially endure. The interpretation that the proposed condition in the amendment would violate the right to a free economic initiative, since persons who otherwise fulfil the other conditions for the performance of a notary function could not be employed because of possible non-compliance with this condition, simply denies the basic principles of bilinguality and bilingual activity in the national mixed area. Such an explanation is not in the spirit of protecting the rights of national communities, which is very high in Slovenia. The essence of a consistent bilingual business would be to ensure that those who decide on the job in this area are also satisfied with this condition. This area is subject to specifications which are historically conditioned and constitutionally suitably regulated. Otherwise, the whole model of bilinguality and multiculturalism, which, of course, applies only to a specific area in the country or to two areas in Primorska and Prekmurje, is in fact denied. In such cases, other public servants may also be asked legitimately why they must meet such conditions. We are very sorry that there was no understanding or consensus on this issue, but I expect the competent ministry, which, by the way, is a judicial one, to find a solution to such issues as soon as possible, based on our assessment of clearly-made errors or partly injustices. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u161	Thank you very much, Dr. Gonch. Parliamentary Group Positive Slovenia, whose position will be presented by Mr Jerič.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-10-24-SDZ6-Redna-18.ana.u162	Thank you so much for saying that. Your Honor! The proposal for a law amending the Notariat Act is a result of the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union, in accordance with which the Republic of Slovenia must remove as soon as possible the conditions of reciprocity in accessing the profession of notary for nationals of the EU Member States, EEC and the Swiss Confederation. The current law on notariat allows foreign citizens access to the profession of notary only on a condition of reciprocity, and such regulation, according to the Court of Justice of the EU, constitutes discrimination based on nationality, which is prohibited by the Treaty of Action of the European Union. In April this year, Slovenia was thus again warned by the European Commission that a new law should be adopted as soon as possible to remove the condition of reciprocity. At the Committee's meeting, we also considered the amendment tabled by the Italian and Hungarian National Communities' Group. The amendment proposed that, in addition to the condition that the Slovenian language should be actively mastered, the candidate should also actively speak Italian or Hungarian language in the areas in which the Italian or Hungarian national communities live. In Positive Slovenia, we could not support such a solution for two reasons. Firstly, since we believe that the current regime is constitutionally appropriate, since the Notariat Act in Article 13 provides that in areas where the official language is also Italian or Hungarian, notary shall consist of notary documents, if the party uses Italian or Hungarian, in both official languages. If the parties or other participants do not understand the language in which the instrument is drawn up, the instrument must contain a clause that their content has been fully translated. He shall translate the notary or, at the request of the party, the interpreter. Secondly, because we believe that such a strict condition could lead to a situation where there would be no such notary in the individual area, as we know, the state is obliged to provide such services to citizens. We will support the proposal for a law amending the Notariat Act in Positive Slovenia. Thank you.
